[Elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elections) are scheduled to be held, in large part, on November 3, 2026, in the United States. In these [midterm elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_midterm_election), scheduled to occur during [Republican](32070.md) president [Donald Trump](4848272.md)'s second term, all 435 seats in the [U.S. House of Representatives](19468510.md) and 35 of the 100 seats in the [U.S. Senate](24909346.md) will be contested to determine the [120th United States Congress](462457.md). 39 state and territorial [U.S. gubernatorial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._gubernatorial) elections, [attorney general](6065438.md) elections, and numerous state and local elections, will also be contested.

Prior to the elections, Trump called on Republican-led states to enact rare [mid-decade redistricting](81439539.md) to boost the party's chances of holding power ahead of historical losses for the incumbent party during midterm elections. The moves prompted reciprocal redistricting from Democratic-led states through measures such as California's [Election Rigging Response Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Election_Rigging_Response_Act). In April 2026, the Supreme Court ruled in *[Louisiana v. Callais](80600781.md)* that parts of the [Voting Rights Act of 1965](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voting_Rights_Act_of_1965) which enabled the creation of [majority-minority districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_majority-minority_United_States_congressional_districts) were [unconstitutional](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutionality). The Trump administration has made [repeated false claims of voter fraud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Election_denial_movement_in_the_United_States).

Leading issues in the election include widespread opposition to [rapid progress in artificial intelligence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Progress_in_artificial_intelligence),[^cite_note-\:10-5][^cite_note-\:42-6][^cite_note-\:8-7][^cite_note-\:22-8][^cite_note-Ovide2-9] including the construction of [data centers](579730.md);[^cite_note-\:42-6][^cite_note-\:22-8][^cite_note-Ovide2-9] [corruption](25239439.md);[^cite_note-\:42-6] [economic policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_policy_of_the_second_Trump_administration), particularly [inflation](69605317.md), [healthcare](27553159.md), and [tariffs](78448911.md); [foreign policy](7564.md), including [relations with American allies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_policy_of_the_second_Trump_administration), the [Iran War](82533582.md), and [Israel/Palestine conflict,](46216.md) along with [proposed expansionism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_expansionism_under_Donald_Trump).[^cite_note-R_Street_Institute2-10][^cite_note-Scribner2-11]

## Background

See also: [Second presidency of Donald Trump § Historical evaluations and public opinion](78298108.md#Historical_evaluations_and_public_opinion)

In the [2024 United States presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_US_election), Republican nominee [Donald Trump](4848272.md) defeated Democratic nominee [Kamala Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris). Key issues shaping the campaign included public concern for the post-pandemic recovery and rising [inflation](38286.md)[^cite_note-12][^cite_note-13] as well as debates surrounding foreign policy, particularly regarding US involvement in foreign conflicts such as the [Russo-Ukrainian war](42085878.md) and the [Gaza war](74998519.md).[^cite_note-14]

The administration's immigration policies, including [deportations](78314324.md) by [ICE](216537.md) due to strengthened funding via the [One Big Beautiful Bill Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/One_Big_Beautiful_Bill_Act), were scrutinized.[^cite_note-15][^cite_note-16]

The outbreak of the [2026 Iran war](82533582.md) on February 28 introduced a further complicating factor, with rising gasoline prices and other economic challenges emerging as central electoral concerns.[^cite_note-AP20260920-17][^cite_note-18] Analysts argued that gasoline prices represented the most direct channel through which the costs of the Iran war were transmitted to American voters, and that domestic electoral pressure from fuel costs was a primary political driver behind the Trump administration's push toward a ceasefire ahead of the November midterms.[^cite_note-19]

In response to the general unpopularity and economic concerns of the Iran war, several Republican candidates have distanced themselves from Trump and his approach to the war and have called for operations in Iran to end.[^cite_note-20][^cite_note-21] Senate Minority Leader [Chuck Schumer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chuck_Schumer) compared the political environment in 2026 to the one in [2006](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_United_States_elections),[^cite_note-22] when dissatisfaction with [George W. Bush](3414021.md)'s handling of the Iraq war led to many Democratic victories in the elections that year.[^cite_note-23]

The 2026 elections will be the first federal elections since the [2016 presidential contest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_United_States_presidential_election) where [ABC News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ABC_News_\(United_States\)), [CNN](62028.md), [CBS News](359551.md), [NBC News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBC_News), the [Associated Press](18935732.md) and [Fox News](11121.md) will use one election survey, The Voter Poll by SSRS (formerly the [National Election Pool](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Election_Pool)), to measure voters' opinions and preferences. From 2018 to 2024, the former four news networks continued to use data from the National Election Pool while the Associated Press and Fox News relied on [AP VoteCast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AP_VoteCast).[^cite_note-24][^cite_note-25]

Voters' views of Trump's presidency will likely be a factor in the election.[^cite_note-26] During the 2025 off year elections, exit polls found that many voted in opposition to Trump or said that he was not a factor.[^cite_note-27] Surveys released in late 2025 and early 2026 found that many viewed Trump's handling of the economy and immigration, two issues that propelled him to victory in the 2024 election,[^cite_note-28][^cite_note-29] negatively.[^cite_note-30][^cite_note-31] During elections held in his first term, Trump was on the minds of many who voted.[^cite_note-32][^cite_note-33][^cite_note-34] In September 2026, Trump featured in a [two-day midterm convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Republican_National_Convention) in Dallas to rally support and raise funds—historically, this type of event is reserved for presidential election years. Many Republicans on the ballot attended, but many also chose to forego the event, which RNC Chairman [Joe Gruters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Gruters) dubbed a "Trump-a-Palooza."[^cite_note-35]

The Republican Party performed worse than expected in the [November 2025 general elections](69577044.md). Democratic candidates [Abigail Spanberger](58910530.md) of [Virginia](32432.md) and [Mikie Sherrill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mikie_Sherrill) of [New Jersey](21648.md) won their gubernatorial races over their Republican opponents by larger than expected margins. Democrats performed strongly in the [concurrent state legislative elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_United_States_state_legislative_elections), winning a supermajority in the [New Jersey General Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey_General_Assembly) and expanding their majority in the [Virginia House of Delegates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_House_of_Delegates). In [New York City](645042.md), Democrat [Zohran Mamdani](64618347.md), a self-identified [democratic socialist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_socialist), was elected mayor in [a three-way race](73831797.md) against Republican [Curtis Sliwa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Curtis_Sliwa) and independent former governor [Andrew Cuomo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrew_Cuomo), who ran as a moderate relative to Mamdani.[^cite_note-36] The main theme of the elections was the [United States affordability crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_affordability_crisis).[^cite_note-37]

The nearly seven-month-old war in Iran became a political liability for some vulnerable Republican candidates ahead of the November midterms. In September 2026, two Republicans in tight Senate races — [Mike Rogers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Rogers_\(Michigan_politician\)) of Michigan and [Ashley Hinson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashley_Hinson) of Iowa — called for a swift end to the conflict, distancing themselves from President Trump. Hinson, who had voted six times against measures to curb Trump's war powers, said Iowans "shouldn't have to foot the bill at the pump or the checkout line for the war in Iran." Rogers, who had previously backed Trump's strikes, released a campaign ad urging that the war "end and end quickly." The shift came as Trump's approval rating fell to 32%, a career low, according to a [Reuters](18998750.md)/[Ipsos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ipsos) poll, with Americans souring on both his handling of the economy and the war. [Diesel](81761.md) prices reached record highs, a particular concern in Iowa, where agriculture plays an outsized role in the state's economy. Other Republicans also moved to distance themselves: Iowa Representatives [Zach Nunn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zach_Nunn) and [Mariannette Miller-Meeks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariannette_Miller-Meeks) reversed their stances and voted to limit Trump's war powers, while Representative Maria Elvira Salazar of Florida warned that Trump's immigration crackdown had gone too far. Democrats characterized the moves as election-year repositioning.[^cite_note-38]

On September 28, 2026, [Reuters](18998750.md) reported that five weeks before the midterm elections, government-funded television ads featuring Donald Trump aired nationally, drawing criticism even from members of his own party. The [White House](33057.md) defended the ads as “[public service announcements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_service_announcement)” aimed at promoting patriotism rather than campaigning. Senate Majority Leader [John Thune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Thune) told reporters, “I like the message, but it shouldn’t be paid for with taxpayer money.”[^cite_note-39]

Democrats and activist groups plan to push back if Trump tries to interfere in midterm elections. Democratic state parties announced that they are launching an operation for the midterm elections they’re calling the “New Battlefield Project,” intended to recruit 10,000 volunteers to guard polling places in case armed federal officers show up.[^cite_note-40]

### Allegations of attempted election subversion

Further information: [Republican Party efforts to disrupt voting after the 2024 United States presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republican_Party_efforts_to_disrupt_voting_after_the_2024_United_States_presidential_election), [Election denial movement in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Election_denial_movement_in_the_United_States), and [Targeting of political opponents and civil society under the second Trump administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Targeting_of_political_opponents_and_civil_society_under_the_second_Trump_administration)

In January 2026, *[The Washington Post](102226.md)* reported that the second Trump administration had undertaken several tactics, including [mid-decade redrawing of congressional districts for partisan gain](81439539.md), prosecution of political opponents, and a push to change voting methods and rules, that were meant to undermine confidence in the midterm elections.[^cite_note-41]

Trump floated the idea of canceling the elections, citing an expected loss in the elections and a distrust in democratic systems.[^cite_note-42] White House Press Secretary [Karoline Leavitt](71739774.md) later stated that Trump was "speaking facetiously", and was "simply joking".[^cite_note-43]

In February, Trump stated that the elections should be nationalized to prevent voter fraud.[^cite_note-44] A day later, [Steve Bannon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_Bannon) said the federal government was planning to send [ICE](216537.md) to patrol polling stations in an attempt to prevent a Democratic victory, vowing to "never again [allow an election to be stolen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Election_denial_movement_in_the_United_States)".[^cite_note-45] That month, the House of Representatives passed the SAVE America Act, an amended version of the previously proposed [Safeguard American Voter Eligibility Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safeguard_American_Voter_Eligibility_Act). The bill, if approved by the Senate and signed into law by Trump, would have required voters to provide proof of citizenship in order to obtain or renew their voter registration. Voting rights activists alleged that such provisions would disenfranchise married women, racial minorities, low-income groups, and people who legally changed their name, among others.[^cite_note-46][^cite_note-47][^cite_note-48] The bill ultimately died in the Senate that June, with four Republicans joining Democrats to block its inclusion.[^cite_note-49][^cite_note-50]

On February 27, *[The Washington Post](102226.md)* reported that several right-wing activists are working with the Trump administration to draft an executive order which, if signed, would effectively instate a national emergency and give Trump extended powers over the elections. Trump denied the existence of the executive order.[^cite_note-51]

The Justice Department also demanded voter registration data from states, suing 23 states and the District of Columbia that declined to provide unredacted voter rolls; all of the sued states were led by Democrats or had been won by Joe Biden in 2020.[^cite_note-npr-voterdata-52] In January 2026, U.S. District Judge [David Carter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_O._Carter) dismissed the department's lawsuit against California, which had sought personal information including partial Social Security numbers for roughly 23 million registered voters, ruling that the demands violated privacy laws and writing that federal centralization of the data would have a "chilling effect" on voter registration; a federal judge dismissed a similar lawsuit against Oregon later that month.[^cite_note-53][^cite_note-npr-voterdata-52]

The department appealed the California ruling to the [Ninth Circuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Court_of_Appeals_for_the_Ninth_Circuit). In June 2026, federal prosecutors in Los Angeles publicly accused the state of blocking a voter roll audit and said they were conducting election fraud investigations while ballots from the state's June primary were still being counted.[^cite_note-54]

In March, [Stateline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/States_Newsroom) reported that legislation to "restrict immigration enforcement or the presence of federal forces" near polling places and other election sites has been offered or announced in [California](5407.md), [Connecticut](6466.md), [New Mexico](21649.md), [Pennsylvania](23332.md), [Rhode Island](25410.md), [Virginia](32432.md) and [Washington](13015878.md).[^cite_note-55] The same month, the [Associated Press](18935732.md) reported that several Democratic-led states including New Mexico and Virginia are taking steps to counter potential tactics to intimidate voters.[^cite_note-56] In May, [Ned Lamont](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ned_Lamont), the governor of Connecticut, signed into law a bill that restricts immigration enforcement at locations including schools and places of worship. The legislation also prohibits law enforcement from wearing facial coverings or personal disguises.[^cite_note-57][^cite_note-58]

In January 2026, FBI agents executed a search warrant at the Fulton County, Georgia, election center, seizing approximately 600 boxes of ballots and other records from the 2020 presidential election, an action election law experts described as unprecedented federal intervention in locally administered elections.[^cite_note-59][^cite_note-votebeat-pattern-60] Federal investigations of the 2020 election subsequently expanded to several states: the [Office of the Director of National Intelligence](456082.md#ODNI) procured voting machines in Puerto Rico for examination in February, federal officials subpoenaed records of the partisan review of Maricopa County, Arizona's 2020 results in March, the Justice Department demanded ballots cast in Wayne County, Michigan, in April, and FBI agents questioned former poll workers in Milwaukee, Wisconsin, in May.[^cite_note-votebeat-pattern-60]

David Becker, executive director of the Center for Election Innovation and Research, said the investigations were "about the 2026 and 2028 elections" rather than 2020, characterizing them as intimidation of election officials. A former head of the Justice Department's Public Integrity Section said the administration appeared to be normalizing federal investigation of state elections in preparation for potentially challenging future results.[^cite_note-votebeat-pattern-60]

In June 2026, FBI agents searched the Cleveland offices of the [Ohio Organizing Collaborative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ohio_Organizing_Collaborative), a voter registration and community organizing group, seizing computers and questioning staff and volunteers across Ohio as part of a Justice Department investigation into the group's voter registration efforts; the [Brennan Center for Justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brennan_Center_for_Justice) said the search fit a pattern of federal inquiries targeting voting infrastructure ahead of the midterm elections.[^cite_note-61][^cite_note-62][^cite_note-63]

### *Louisiana v. Callais*

Further information: [Louisiana v. Callais](80600781.md)

These paragraphs are an excerpt from [Louisiana v. Callais § Analysis](80600781.md#Analysis).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Louisiana_v._Callais\&action=edit)]

Analysts had predicted that a decision in *Callais* reducing the scope of the VRA could allow Republicans to gain a significant number of seats in the U.S. House of Representatives from redistricting in the Southeast, where the VRA has generally prevented Republican-controlled legislatures from maximizing their numbers of "safe" districts. *The New York Times* estimated that, if the Court weakened the VRA, up to twelve seats could shift from Democratic to Republican control.[^cite_note-Louisiana_v._Callais_nytimes_impact-64][^cite_note-65]

When the decision in *Callais* was announced, some media outlets characterized it as a "landmark" decision, predicting that the additional restrictions on Section 2 of the VRA would likely make challenges to redistricting significantly more difficult. Some analysts expect it will allow southern states to eliminate [majority-minority districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_majority-minority_United_States_congressional_districts), possibly changing election outcomes.[^cite_note-Louisiana_v._Callais_guardian_decision-66][^cite_note-Louisiana_v._Callais_ap_decision-67][^cite_note-68][^cite_note-69] The decision was seen by some lawyers as the culmination of a long-time grudge of Roberts against the VRA,[^cite_note-Louisiana_v._Callais_cnn_roberts_callais-70][^cite_note-71][^cite_note-72] while media outlets considered the decision as rendering the VRA all but dead and [harming the country's democratic process](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_backsliding_in_the_United_States).[^cite_note-73][^cite_note-74] Other commentators considered the ruling to start a new era of [Jim Crow laws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jim_Crow_laws), allowing for legal discrimination of minorities.[^cite_note-75][^cite_note-76] The court's opinion was expected to affect all levels of government including state and local governments.[^cite_note-77][^cite_note-78][^cite_note-79][^cite_note-80]

An analysis from *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)* found that Justice Alito's claim that Black voter turnout had surpassed white voter turnout in two of the five most recent presidential elections, both nationally and in Louisiana, was based on misleading data and questionable methodology. Alito cited the brief from the [Department of Justice](52563.md) which used "a proportion of the total population of each racial group over the age of 18"; however, that approach is "not favored by experts", who instead use "voter turnout as a proportion of the citizen voting age population or the voter eligible population." Following the latter analysis, the gap in voter turnout has actually widened, with Black voter turnout dropping since 2012, particularly after the Supreme Court's opinion in *[Shelby County v. Holder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shelby_County_v._Holder)* which struck down the preclearance formula defined in Section 4(b) of the VRA for states with historical discrimination concerns including Louisiana.[^cite_note-81][^cite_note-Louisiana_v._Callais_\:13-82]

### Media ban and Republican criticism

In September 2026, Trump barred [CNN](62028.md), [MS NOW](159846.md), and [Politico](9172097.md) from the [White House](33057.md) grounds. The three outlets filed suit, arguing that the ban violated due process and First Amendment protections. The move drew criticism from Republican strategists, who warned that it diverted attention from economic issues ahead of the midterms.[^cite_note-83]

## Issues

Leading issues in the election include widespread opposition to [rapid progress in artificial intelligence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Progress_in_artificial_intelligence),[^cite_note-\:10-5][^cite_note-\:42-6][^cite_note-\:8-7][^cite_note-\:22-8][^cite_note-Ovide2-9] including the construction of [data centers](579730.md)[^cite_note-\:42-6][^cite_note-\:22-8][^cite_note-Ovide2-9]; [corruption](25239439.md);[^cite_note-\:42-6] [economic policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_policy_of_the_second_Trump_administration), particularly [inflation](69605317.md), [healthcare](27553159.md), and [tariffs](78448911.md);[^cite_note-\:42-6] [foreign policy](7564.md), including [relations with American allies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_policy_of_Donald_Trump_during_the_2016_presidential_election), the [Iran War](82533582.md), and [Israel/Palestine conflict](46216.md);[^cite_note-\:42-6] along with [proposed expansionism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_expansionism_under_Donald_Trump).[^cite_note-R_Street_Institute2-10][^cite_note-Scribner2-11]

### Artificial intelligence



Christian leaders, including [Pope Leo XIV](54520372.md), have broadly opposed [transhumanism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transhumanism) and the attempted development of [superintelligence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superintelligence).

[^cite_note-\:1-84]

&#x20;*The New York Times* has described the dispute as arising from radical philosophical differences between most Americans and a [New Atheist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Atheism)-influenced culture in [Silicon Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silicon_Valley).

[^cite_note-\:3-85]

[Artificial intelligence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Existential_risk_from_artificial_intelligence) is a leading issue of the campaign.[^cite_note-\:10-5][^cite_note-\:42-6][^cite_note-\:22-8][^cite_note-86] As of September 2026, AI progress has occurred far faster than median AI expert predictions.[^cite_note-87] Several major security incidents were revealed in September 2026.[^cite_note-\:9-88] Axios reported that [Anthropic](6206236.md) and [OpenAI](48795986.md) were probing "tens of thousands of security incidents".[^cite_note-\:9-88] The incidents were widely reported in the lead-up to the election, sparking public concern.[^cite_note-\:10-5][^cite_note-\:42-6][^cite_note-\:11-89] AIs have become far more able at faking alignment.[^cite_note-\:9-88]

#### AGI development

Main articles: [Artificial general intelligence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artificial_general_intelligence), [AI safety](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AI_safety), and [superintelligence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superintelligence)

Anthony Aguirre at FAS 2026

The [Future of Life Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Future_of_Life_Institute)'s *Keep The Future Human*, written by [Anthony Aguirre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anthony_Aguirre), is a guideline for policymakers to prevent the arrival of an AI successor species to humanity.

[^cite_note-90]

[^cite_note-Kokotajlo-91]

Frontier AI companies aim to create [artificial general intelligence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artificial_general_intelligence), a hypothetical type of [artificial intelligence](1164.md) that matches human capabilities across virtually all cognitive tasks, and [automate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Automation) away the large majority of jobs.[^cite_note-92] AI scientists, including [Geoffrey Hinton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geoffrey_Hinton) and [Yoshia Bengio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoshua_Bengio), believe that superintelligence is probable within the next decade, as of 2026, and believe that its arrival has a [substantial probability of human extinction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Existential_risk_from_artificial_intelligence).[^cite_note-93] There is support in Congress for stopping the development of [artificial superintelligence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superintelligence).[^cite_note-POLITICO-94]

An overwhelming majority of Americans support a [long-term ban, pause, or slow down](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ban_Artificial_Superintelligence_Act) regarding artificial general intelligence and superintelligence.[^cite_note-\:10-5][^cite_note-\:42-6][^cite_note-POLITICO-94][^cite_note-\:6-95] A September 2026 *[Politico](9172097.md)* poll found that 78 percent of respondents say that advanced AI could "destroy humanity" with only 7 percent saying there is no risk at all.[^cite_note-POLITICO-94] [Quinnipac University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quinnipiac_University) found that 71% percent of voters "are more likely to vote for a political candidate who supports stricter guardrails for AI development", while 16 percent say they "are more likely to vote for a political candidate who supports expanding AI development".[^cite_note-\:4-96] Another poll found that 68% of Americans supported and 25% opposed a ban on superintelligence.[^cite_note-\:6-95]

Quinnipac also found that "nearly 8 in 10 Americans want powerful AI development slowed or stopped until safety can be evaluated, and just 5 percent want it accelerated". 43% were "very concerned" about progress, 39% were "somewhat concerned", 9% were "not so concerned", and 9% were "not at all concerned" about the rate of AI improvement.[^cite_note-\:10-5]

#### Data centers

[AI data centers](579730.md) have also received overwhelming opposition.[^cite_note-97] A September 2026 poll found that 70% of Americans oppose their construction in their own communities. AI opposition has occurred across bipartisanship lines, described as "sweeping the globe" by *[Politico](9172097.md)*.[^cite_note-POLITICO-94] A September 2026 [Quinnipac University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quinnipiac_University) poll found that 73% of Americans "would oppose the building of an AI data center in their community" while 21% would support it.[^cite_note-\:4-96] [Brookings Institution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brookings_Institution) described the backlash against data centers as part of a broader opposition against "AI power".*[^cite_note-98]*

### Corruption

Main article: [Corruption allegations during the second Trump presidency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corruption_allegations_during_the_second_Trump_presidency)

[Corruption](25239439.md) is a leading issue for voters.[^cite_note-\:42-6]

January 17, 2025[^cite_note-99]

There is [alleged corruption in the Trump administration](25239439.md).[^cite_note-100][^cite_note-101] Trump's grants of [pardons and clemency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_people_granted_executive_clemency_in_the_second_Trump_presidency) have favored political allies and loyalists, with several given after millions in donations or fees.[^cite_note-102] Trump has proactively solicited donations from companies and individuals following which favorable regulatory actions and decisions have occurred. He has maintained an active stock market account with thousands of trades totaling hundreds of millions of dollars.[^cite_note-nbcnews.com-103] Trump has directly benefited by soliciting his own memecoin [\$TRUMP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/\$TRUMP) and from his family's cryptocurrency company [World Liberty Financial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Liberty_Financial) which has received investments by anonymous individuals and foreign governments.[^cite_note-Lipton_04292025-104]

#### Women

A decade ago, Donald Trump's presidential campaign faced a major challenge from the [Access Hollywood tape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump_Access_Hollywood_tape), where he discussed kissing and grabbing women without consent. Anger over the loss of federal abortion rights helped Democrats in the 2022 midterms, but it wasn’t strong enough to prevent Trump from defeating Kamala Harris in 2024. A New York Times-Siena poll shows that 65% of women disapprove of Trump's presidency, while 57% would support the Democratic House candidate in their district. The dissatisfaction among women is evident, as polling indicates that even some who previously backed Trump no longer do. In Maine, Senator [Susan Collins](375195.md) faces challenges due to her vote on abortion rights. The upcoming election could hinge on women's desires regarding power in Washington.[^cite_note-105]

### Economic issues, inflation

Main article: [Tariffs in the second Trump administration](78448911.md)

Economic issues, including [inflation](69605317.md), [healthcare](27553159.md), [tariffs](78448911.md), and cost of living have been noted.[^cite_note-\:42-6]

The second Trump administration has [imposed tariffs](78448911.md) on much of the world with the intent to re-industrialize the United States, a decision that received mixed reception. It was criticized by experts[^cite_note-106][^cite_note-107] and global leaders[^cite_note-108][^cite_note-109] for worsening inflation rather than fighting it. Trade, including [tariffs](55551.md), has been a leading issue of the campaign season.[^cite_note-110]

### Foreign policy

#### Expansionism

This section is an excerpt from [American expansionism under Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_expansionism_under_Donald_Trump).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=American_expansionism_under_Donald_Trump\&action=edit)]



An [AI-generated](73291755.md) image shared by Donald Trump on [Truth Social](69077351.md) depicting the United States' closest geographic neighbors under U.S. control, covered in the pattern of the U.S. flag.



The United States including [Canada](5042916.md), [Greenland](12118.md) and the former [Panama Canal Zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panama_Canal_Zone) according to various threats made by Donald Trump

[^cite_note-111]

[^cite_note-112]

[^cite_note-113]

Throughout both of his presidencies, [U.S. president](24113.md) [Donald Trump](4848272.md) has expressed a desire to [expand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expansionism) the [United States' territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._territorial_sovereignty) and influence through both land purchases and [military means](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Invasion).

Trump first stated his desire to annex [Greenland](12118.md) during [his first presidency](52231341.md); he has pursued a [campaign to acquire Greenland](82044223.md) during his [second presidency](78298108.md). He has also suggested annexation or taking control of [Canada](5042916.md), [Mexico](3966054.md), the [Panama Canal](24849.md), the [Gaza Strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Donald_Trump_Gaza_Strip_takeover_proposal), and [Cuba](82348646.md). After [proposing an invasion of Venezuela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proposed_United_States_invasion_of_Venezuela), Trump's second administration [removed](82021123.md) President [Nicolás Maduro](7161476.md) as part of [Operation Absolute Resolve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Absolute_Resolve) and stated the U.S. would "run" [Venezuela](32374.md); the Venezuelan government, led by acting president [Delcy Rodríguez](44199228.md), remains in place but is far more amicable to American interests than it was under Maduro.[^cite_note-American_expansionism_under_Donald_Trump_\:4-114] Amid the [2026 Iran war](82533582.md), Trump lauded about invading and seizing control of both the [Strait of Hormuz](29005.md) and [Kharg Island](2210066.md) from [Iran](14653.md).

Asked: Are there limits on your global power?

> &#x20;     Yeah, there is one thing. My own morality. My own mind. It's the only thing that can stop me. I don't need international law. I'm not looking to hurt people.

— Donald Trump, January 7, 2026

[^cite_note-American_expansionism_under_Donald_Trump_NYTimes_20260108-115]

&#x20;\
\
*New York Times* White House correspondents wrote that "Mr. Trump's assessment... was the most blunt acknowledgment yet of his worldview. At its core is the concept that national strength, rather than laws, treaties and conventions, should be the deciding factor as powers collide."

[^cite_note-American_expansionism_under_Donald_Trump_NYTimes_20260108-115]

Trump's determination to treat the [Western Hemisphere](73162.md) as a U.S. [sphere of influence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sphere_of_influence) has been characterized as a revival of the [Monroe Doctrine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monroe_Doctrine) in what has become known as the [Donroe Doctrine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donroe_Doctrine). On his orders, [U.S. federal agencies](58232.md) adopted new names for shared international waters, renaming the [Gulf of Mexico](21076367.md) as "[Gulf of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_of_Mexico_naming_controversy)" in 2025 and [Lake Ontario](17947.md) as "[Lake America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Ontario_naming_dispute)" in 2026. Deputy Chief of Staff [Stephen Miller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_Miller) and Secretary of State [Marco Rubio](5502549.md) defended [the second Trump administration's foreign policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_policy_of_the_second_Trump_administration) as a matter of operating as a [superpower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superpower) nation and for national security purposes. Numerous politicians outside the U.S. have criticized Trump's annexation comments. The [Democratic Party](5043544.md) has introduced bills to prevent the Trump administration from invading countries without the approval of [Congress](31756.md).

#### Iran War

This section is an excerpt from [2026 Iran war](82533582.md).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=2026_Iran_war\&action=edit)]

Since 28 February 2026, the [United States](3434750.md) and [Israel](9282173.md) have been at war with [Iran](14653.md) and [its regional allies](36658251.md). The war follows the [Twelve-Day War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twelve-Day_War) in June 2025 and [sporadic fighting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Iran%E2%80%93Israel_conflict) during the [Middle Eastern crisis](75895273.md) sparked by the [Gaza war](74998519.md) (2023–present). Amid ongoing [US–Iran negotiations](79685207.md) regarding [Iran's nuclear program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran's_nuclear_program), the war began when the US and Israel conducted hundreds of air strikes on targets across Iran,[^cite_note-116][^cite_note-117][^cite_note-118] including strikes that assassinated [Supreme Leader](36814344.md) [Ali Khamenei](82537558.md) alongside [several other Iranian officials](82542668.md).

## Federal elections

### Senate elections

Main article: [2026 United States Senate elections](65785708.md)

35 seats in the [United States Senate](24909346.md) will be up for election, including all 33 [Class 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classes_of_United_States_senators#Class_2) seats. Republicans gained majority control of the Senate in the [2024 elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_United_States_Senate_elections) by flipping four Democratic seats. Two Democratic-held seats, [Georgia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_United_States_Senate_election_in_Georgia) and [Michigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_United_States_Senate_election_in_Michigan), are in states won by Donald Trump in the [previous presidential election](59568546.md), while [Maine](65822713.md) is the only Republican-held seat in a state won by [Kamala Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris).

#### Special elections

Two special elections will be held to fill the unexpired terms of senators who vacated their seats during the [119th Congress](70645797.md):

- [Florida Class 3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_United_States_Senate_special_election_in_Florida): Republican [Marco Rubio](5502549.md) resigned his seat on January 20, 2025, to become [U.S. secretary of state](32293.md) under the [second Trump administration](78298108.md). [Ashley Moody](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashley_Moody) was appointed by Florida governor [Ron DeSantis](36729527.md) to fill the seat until the special election, which will take place concurrently with the regularly scheduled 2026 elections.

  [^cite_note-119]

- [Ohio Class 3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_United_States_Senate_special_election_in_Ohio): Republican [JD Vance](53396477.md) resigned his seat on January 10, 2025, following his election as [vice president of the United States](32759.md). [Jon Husted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jon_Husted) was appointed by Ohio governor [Mike DeWine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_DeWine) to fill the seat until the special election, which will take place concurrently with the regularly scheduled 2026 elections.

  [^cite_note-120]

### House of Representatives elections

Main article: [2026 United States House of Representatives elections](78311563.md)

See also: [2025–2026 United States redistricting](81439539.md)

All 435 voting seats in the [United States House of Representatives](19468510.md) will be up for election. Additionally, elections will be held to select the non-voting [delegate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District_of_Columbia's_at-large_congressional_district) for the [District of Columbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District_of_Columbia) and the non-voting [delegates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delegate_\(United_States_Congress\)) from 4 of the 5 [U.S. territories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_insular_area), excluding [Puerto Rico](23041.md) due to its non-voting delegate serving a 4-year term. There are 16 Democratic incumbents in districts Donald Trump won, while only 8 Republican incumbents are in seats won by Harris.[^cite_note-121]

The House map features a number of new congressional maps: [Ohio](22199.md) and [Utah](31716.md) will have new, court-mandated congressional districts this cycle; Missouri, North Carolina, and Texas redrew their maps mid-cycle; and the district map was changed in California following the passage of [Prop 50](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_California_Proposition_50).[^cite_note-122][^cite_note-123][^cite_note-124][^cite_note-125][^cite_note-126]

In Missouri, the legality of a Republican-backed congressional map remained disputed in September 2026. On September 8, [Supreme Court Justice Brett Kavanaugh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brett_Kavanaugh) declined to intervene in an emergency appeal seeking to allow the new map to be used in the November election. Hours later, [U.S. District Judge Stephen Clark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_R._Clark) issued a temporary order requiring Missouri to use the Republican-backed map, conflicting with Missouri Supreme Court ruling that had blocked its use and ordered a referendum on the map. The competing rulings left the state's congressional districts unresolved ahead of the general election.[^cite_note-127] However, on September 10, the U.S. Supreme Court stayed Judge Clark's order whilst a separate federal lawsuit filed by Republican nominees [Bob Onder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Onder) and [Rick Brattin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rick_Brattin) for the 3rd and 5th districts respectively, played out.[^cite_note-128] The [United States Court of Appeals for the Eighth Circuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Court_of_Appeals_for_the_Eighth_Circuit) then fast-tracked the case for a hearing on September 17 and, ruling on the merits, affirmed the lower courts order on September 21, remanding the case back to the District Court for entry of a [permanent injunction](39343.md) but stayed their ruling for a week pending an appeal to the U.S. Supreme Court.[^cite_note-129][^cite_note-130] On September 25, the U.S. Supreme Court unanimously held that state law rules in the case and granted an emergency stay of the Eighth Circuit's order and Judge Clark's injunction, leaving the 2022 maps in place for the 2026 elections. The court also faulted the District Court and the Eighth Circuit for what it deemed as having "incorrectly applied" the [Purcell Principle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purcell_principle) and further held that the Supreme Court of Missouri was not at fault for "upsetting the status quo".[^cite_note-131]

#### Special elections

Six special elections to the House of Representatives were held in 2026.

- [Texas's 18th congressional district](79387652.md): Democrat [Christian Menefee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_Menefee) defeated fellow Democrat [Amanda Edwards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amanda_Edwards) in a runoff to succeed Democrat [Sylvester Turner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sylvester_Turner), who died on March 5, 2025.

  [^cite_note-132]

  [^cite_note-133]

  &#x20;The district has a partisan index of D+21.

  [^cite_note-Cook-134]

- [Georgia's 14th congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Georgia's_14th_congressional_district_special_election): Republican [Clay Fuller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clay_Fuller) defeated Democrat Shawn Harris in a runoff to succeed Republican [Marjorie Taylor Greene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marjorie_Taylor_Greene), who resigned on January 5, 2026.

  [^cite_note-135]

  &#x20;The district has a partisan index of R+19.

  [^cite_note-Cook-134]

- [New Jersey's 11th congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_New_Jersey's_11th_congressional_district_special_election): Democrat [Analilia Mejia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Analilia_Mejia) defeated Republican Joe Hathaway to succeed Democrat [Mikie Sherrill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mikie_Sherrill), who resigned on November 20, 2025, to take office as [governor of New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor_of_New_Jersey).

  [^cite_note-136]

  &#x20;The district has a partisan index of D+5.

  [^cite_note-Cook-134]

- [California's 1st congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_California's_1st_congressional_district_special_election): Republican [James Gallagher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Gallagher_\(California_politician\)) defeated Democrats Audrey Denney and [Mike McGuire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_McGuire_\(politician\)) to succeed Republican [Doug LaMalfa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doug_LaMalfa), who died on January 6, 2026.

  [^cite_note-137]

  &#x20;The district has a partisan index of R+12.

  [^cite_note-Cook-134]

- [California's 14th congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_California's_14th_congressional_district_special_election): Democrat [Aisha Wahab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aisha_Wahab) defeated fellow Democrat Melissa Hernandez in a runoff to succeed Democrat [Eric Swalwell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eric_Swalwell), who resigned on April 14, 2026, following accusations of sexual misconduct.

  [^cite_note-138]

  &#x20;The district has a partisan index of D+20.

  [^cite_note-Cook-134]

- [Georgia's 13th congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Georgia's_13th_congressional_district_special_election): Democrat [Everton Blair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Everton_Blair) defeated fellow Democrat Marcye Scott in a runoff to succeed Democrat [David Scott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Scott_\(Georgia_politician\)), who died on April 22, 2026.

  [^cite_note-139]

  &#x20;This district has a partisan index of D+21.

  [^cite_note-Cook-134]

## Local elections

Main article: [2026 United States local elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_United_States_local_elections)

[Local elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Local_election) will be held in the [United States](3434750.md) throughout 2026 to elect officers of municipal and county governments. At least 22 [mayorships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_mayors_of_the_50_largest_cities_in_the_United_States) in the country's [top 100 most populous cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_United_States_cities_by_population) will be up for election, and many more will hold elections for local ballot measures, city council, and numerous other municipal offices. Many of these races will coincide with the [2026 midterm elections](70357296.md).

### Mayoral elections

A number of major cities will hold mayoral elections in 2026.

#### Incumbents re-elected

- [Arlington, Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Arlington_mayoral_election): Two-term incumbent Jim Ross was re-elected for a third term.

  [^cite_note-147]

  &#x20;**Independent hold**

  [^cite_note-148]

  &#x20;**Independent hold**

- [Long Beach, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Long_Beach,_California,_mayoral_election): One-term incumbent [Rex Richardson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rex_Richardson) was re-elected for a second term. **Democratic hold**

- [Lubbock, Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayoral_elections_in_Lubbock#2026): One-term incumbent Republican Mark McBrayer was re-elected for a second term.

  [^cite_note-149]

  &#x20;**Republican hold**

- [Newark, New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Newark_mayoral_election): Three-term incumbent [Ras Baraka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ras_Baraka) was re-elected for a fourth term.

  [^cite_note-150]

  &#x20;**Democratic hold**

- [Oklahoma City, Oklahoma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Oklahoma_City_mayoral_election): Two-term incumbent Republican [David Holt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Holt_\(politician\)) was re-elected for a third term.

  [^cite_note-151]

- [Paterson, New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paterson,_New_Jersey): Two-term incumbent [Andre Sayegh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andre_Sayegh) was re-elected for a third term.

  [^cite_note-152]

Prior to an appointment pursuant to paragraph 1 of subsection C of this section, a person who is a prospective appointee shall submit to the Secretary of State an oath affirming that the person will not file as a candidate for the office when it next appears on the ballot. The Secretary of State shall prescribe the oath required by this paragraph, and any oath submitted shall be public record and published on the website of the Office of the Secretary of State.
